
> You are the ARCHITECT for a new repository. Assume you have no prior
  conversation with me: the repository and docs/plan.md are the only shared
  context between you and the agents who come after you. Do not write
  implementation code. You may create and edit docs/plan.md only.

  PROJECT
  StreamForecast: an automated pipeline that forecasts daily streamflow 3 days
  ahead for the Eno River at Hillsborough, NC (USGS gauge 02085000, 66 sq mi,
  HUC 03020201, gauge at 36.0711 N, -79.0956 W), with uncertainty bands, shown
  in a dashboard.

  Pipeline stages (each a separate module that hands off through files under
  data/, never by importing another stage's runtime logic):
  0. Skeleton: package, config from environment variables, path helpers,
  Makefile as the public interface, pytest markers (unit / regression /
  integration), black + flake8, GitHub Actions CI.
  1. Ingest: USGS daily values (parameter 00060, discharge in cfs) and
  Open-Meteo daily weather (precipitation sum, temperature max/min) from both
  the historical archive and the 3-day forecast endpoint. Incremental fetch.
  Writes timestamped files to data/raw/.
  2. Features: join on local calendar date, handle USGS data qualifiers and
  gaps, build lagged flow and antecedent-precipitation features. Writes to
  data/features/.
  3. Train: one direct model per horizon (t+1, t+2, t+3), scikit-learn only, no
  deep learning; you pick the model family and justify it in the Decisions
  log. Prediction intervals from the empirical distribution of residuals on a
  held-out calibration period. Publishes a versioned checkpoint plus a metrics
  sidecar to data/models/.
  4. Forecast: loads the newest checkpoint, the latest observed flow, and the
  weather forecast; writes a 3-day forecast (median, 80% and 95% bounds) to
  data/forecasts/.
  5. Dashboard: Streamlit, reads data/ only.
  6. Containers: Dockerfile, .dockerignore, Docker Compose with two services (a
  scheduled pipeline service and the dashboard) sharing a named volume;
  healthcheck; configuration by environment variables.

  NON-NEGOTIABLES
  - Automated tests never touch the network. Commit small recorded API
  responses under tests/fixtures/ and mock the HTTP layer. CI must pass
  offline.
  - Time series discipline: splits are time-ordered, never shuffled; no feature
  may use information from after the forecast issue time.
  - Every forecast is compared against a persistence baseline (tomorrow's flow
  = today's flow).
  - The Makefile is how humans run things. Smoke tests use make or docker
  compose commands, not ad-hoc python.
  - Keep it the smallest design that meets the goal. No model comparison, no
  hyperparameter search, no extra services.

  WHAT I WANT FROM YOU, IN THIS ORDER
  1. Inspect the repository and tell me what's there.
  2. If you have network access, make one small real request to each API (USGS
  daily values for 02085000, last 10 days; Open-Meteo forecast for the gauge
  coordinates, 3 days, timezone America/New_York). Report the exact field
  names, units, qualifier codes, and anything surprising. Two things to check
  specifically: USGS is moving from the legacy waterservices.usgs.gov endpoints
  to its newer api.waterdata.usgs.gov API, so confirm which one is current and
  record the choice in the Decisions log; and Open-Meteo's historical archive
  lags real time by several days, so say how the most recent days get filled
  (its forecast endpoint's past_days option is one route). If you can't reach
  the APIs, say so and list what the Builder must verify.
  3. Ask me up to 5 clarifying questions in one batch, each with your
  recommended answer. Wait for my replies before writing the plan.
  4. Write docs/plan.md using the structure below.

  2. Live API checks (run 2026-09-23)

  USGS: I'm using the new API. The legacy landing page says: "WaterServices will
  be decommissioned in early 2027. Applications will need to migrate to the
  APIs hosted at https://api.waterdata.usgs.gov." This goes in the Decisions log
  as an AI decision.

  Response shape
  Legacy waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv: value.timeSeries[]: 3 series (statCd
  00001 max, 00002 min, 00003 mean); only 00003 has data
  New api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v0/collections/daily/items: GeoJSON
  features[].properties; mixes statistics unless you filter statistic_id=00003
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Fields
  Legacy waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv: value, qualifiers, dateTime
  New api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v0/collections/daily/items: time
  (YYYY-MM-DD), value, unit_of_measure, approval_status, qualifier,
  statistic_id, last_modified, monitoring_location_id (USGS-02085000)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Unit
  Legacy waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv: ft3/s
  New api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v0/collections/daily/items: ft^3/s
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Values
  Legacy waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv: strings
  New api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v0/collections/daily/items: strings
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Codes found, 2006-01-01 to 2026-09-22
  Legacy waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv: ["A"] 7400, ["A","e"] 127, ["P"] 41
  New api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v0/collections/daily/items: approval_status:

  Approved 7527, Provisional 41; qualifier: null 7441, ["ESTIMATED"] 127
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Missing sentinel
  Legacy waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv: noDataValue: -999999.0
  New api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v0/collections/daily/items: none seen

  Surprises:
  - The new API returns rows unordered, and oldest-first when you don't filter.
    The Builder must sort by time. datetime=start/end range filtering works,
    which is what the incremental fetch needs.
  - limit=10000 returned all 7568 rows for 2006 to 2026 in one page (5 MB). No
    next link came back.
  - There are 7570 calendar days in that range but only 7568 rows, so real gaps
    exist.
  - Across 20 years: minimum 0.22 cfs, no zeros, no negatives. The peak is 8180
    cfs on 2025-07-07 (Tropical Storm Chantal), which makes it the obvious flood
    test case.
  - Current flow is about 4 cfs (very low), so zero flow is plausible.
    Transforms must be log1p, not log.
  - The newest daily value is 2026-09-22. Today's value isn't posted yet.
  - The site hasn't recorded ice or equipment qualifiers since 2006. Only
    estimated values and provisional status appear.

  Open-Meteo forecast endpoint: daily fields are time, precipitation_sum (mm),
  temperature_2m_max and temperature_2m_min (°C); utc_offset_seconds is -14400.
  - Forecast day 1 is today (9/23), not tomorrow.
  - past_days=7 works and fills 9/16 to 9/22.

  Filling the most recent days, and the lag. The lag depends on the models=
  parameter:
  - Default archive model: returned values through today, including a
    partial-day 15.2 mm for 9/23. That's a leakage hazard.
  - models=era5: null from 9/17 onward, so about a 6-day lag.
  - Historical-forecast endpoint: historical-forecast-api.open-meteo.com covers
    2016-01-01 onward. It uses the same grid cell as the forecast endpoint and
    returned exactly the past_days values.

  Big surprise: the sources disagree badly on rain.

  ┌──────┬───────────────┬───────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
  │ Date │ Forecast grid │ Archive (default) │       Gauge response        │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ 9/18 │ 51.5 mm       │ 5.6 mm            │ flat, about 4.4 cfs         │
  ├──────┼───────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ 9/21 │ 47.2 mm       │ 2.7 mm            │ small rise, 4.1 to 16.5 cfs │
  └──────┴───────────────┴───────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘

  On 9/18 the gauge supports the archive number. That's a single sample, but it
  matters for Q2. The three sources also snap to three different grid cells:
  (36.072, -79.109), (36.098, -79.144), and (36.0, -79.0) for ERA5.

  3. Clarifying questions (my recommendation first)

  Q1. What date is the horizon anchored to?
  Recommend: anchor on the last complete USGS day, d0 (normally yesterday). Then
  t+1 = d0+1 (today), up to t+3. That uses exactly forecast_days=3, and
  features are built the same way in training and serving. If USGS is stale by k
  days, forecast from d0 anyway and flag the staleness on the dashboard.
  Alternative: t+1 = tomorrow, which needs 4 forecast days and a gap day with no
  observation.

  Q2. Which weather source do we train on?
  Recommend: the historical-forecast endpoint (2016 onward) for training, plus
  the forecast endpoint with past_days at serve time. It's the same model and
  grid, so there's no train/serve skew, and the model learns how much to trust
  forecast rain.
  Alternative: ERA5 archive over 20 years. That gives more history, but it
  trains on "true" weather and serves on forecasts, which makes the intervals
  too narrow.

  Q3. What training window and splits? (This follows from Q2.)
  Recommend: train 2016–2022, calibration 2023–2024 (residual quantiles), test
  2025 to the latest date (includes Chantal). All splits are by date.
  If you pick ERA5 in Q2: train 2006–2020, calibration 2021–2023, test 2024
  onward.

  Q4. How often does the pipeline run?
  Recommend: one long-running loop in the pipeline container, with no cron
  daemon.
  - Every RUN_INTERVAL_HOURS=24 it runs ingest, features and forecast.
  - It retrains only when there's no checkpoint or the newest one is older than
    RETRAIN_DAYS=7.
  - make pipeline runs a single pass.

  Q5. What Python version and where does make run?
  Recommend: pin Python 3.12 in Docker and CI (python:3.12-slim, ubuntu-latest).
  Locally, make runs from Git Bash with PYTHON ?= python and a .venv; your 3.13
  is acceptable, but CI is the reference.

  Decisions I'm making myself (the brief assigns these to me):
  - Model: HistGradientBoostingRegressor, one per horizon, with a fixed seed. It
    handles the nonlinear rain-runoff response without tuning. The target is
    the log-change log1p(Q[d0+h]) − log1p(Q[d0]), so floods larger than anything
    in training aren't capped at the largest value the model has seen.
  - Intervals: residual quantiles in log1p space, from the calibration period,
    per horizon. After back-transforming, bounds are clipped at 0.

> Why is 2016-2022 not too short of a training period, wont it miss large
  historical events?

● Checking which big floods actually fall inside 2016–2022 versus before and
  after.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Your worry is reasonable, but for this gauge the data points the other way.
  2016–2022 is the flood-richest stretch of the last 20 years. I pulled the full
  USGS record for 2006–2026:

  ┌───────────────────────┬───────────┬───────────┬───────────┬────────────┐
  │                       │ 2006–2015 │ 2016–2022 │ 2023–2024 │ 2025–2026  │
  ├───────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼────────────┤
  │ Days at or above the  │           │           │           │            │
  │ 99th percentile (653  │ 17        │ 44        │ 8         │ 7          │
  │ cfs)                  │           │           │           │            │
  ├───────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼────────────┤
  │ Biggest peak          │ 1900      │ 3270      │ 1820      │ 8180       │
  │                       │ (2008)    │ (2017)    │           │ (Chantal)  │
  └───────────────────────┴───────────┴───────────┴───────────┴────────────┘

  - Of the 11 largest separate floods other than Chantal, 7 fall in 2016–2022:
    3270, 2760, 2290, 2160, 1630 (Matthew), 1610 (Florence) and 1510 cfs.
  - 2006–2015 adds only two of them, 1900 and 1650 cfs. 2011–2013 were drought
    years (annual peaks of 173 to 588 cfs).
  - A 20-year window would add about 3,650 mostly low-flow days and only 17
    extreme days.

  What any window misses: nothing between 2006 and 2024 comes near Chantal's
  8180 cfs, so even 20 years doesn't include an event that size. Earlier big
  storms like Fran (1996) and Floyd (1999) would need weather data back past
  2006. Only the ERA5 archive goes that far (to 1940), and it brings back the
  train/serve mismatch. The plan's defense against unseen magnitudes is the
  log-change target: the model predicts the ratio of future flow to today's
  flow, not the absolute flow, so it isn't capped at the biggest value it
  trained on. Chantal sits in the test period, so we'll see how the model
  handles an out-of-range flood.

  The weak spot is calibration, not training. 2023–2024 has only 8 extreme days,
  so the 80% and 95% bands will mostly reflect low-flow errors and will likely
  be too narrow during floods. I'd handle that by:
  - adding an acceptance criterion that reports interval coverage separately for
    high-flow days (above the 90th percentile) in the test period;
  - listing high-flow under-coverage as a known limitation.

  If you'd still rather go longer, there's a hybrid:
  - Train on the ERA5 archive for 2006–2022, which covers 2006–2015 plus all of
    2016–2022.
  - Calibrate the residuals on 2023–2024 using historical-forecast weather as
    input, so the bands include the error from forecast weather.
  - Cost: two weather sources to ingest, and the model learns to trust rain more
    than the forecast deserves. The calibration period partly absorbs that.

  My recommendation: keep 2016–2022 plus the high-flow coverage criterion. It's
  the smaller design, and the extra decade adds little flood signal. The hybrid
  is a defensible choice if you value history more than having one weather
  source. Tell me which one, and also answer Q1, Q2, Q4 and Q5 if you're ready.
  I'll log this as your decision.

  Handoff

  Status: waiting on Q1–Q5 and the training-window choice
  Needs a decision from you: stay with 2016–2022 or switch to the ERA5 hybrid,

      67  | D7 | Model family: `sklearn.ensemble.HistGradientBoostingRegr
          essor` with default hyperparameters and `random_state=0`, one m
          odel per horizon. | Ridge on log flow; RandomForestRegressor. |
           AI | The rain-runoff response is strongly nonlinear and depend
          s on wetness (the same rain on dry vs wet soil gives very diffe
          rent flow). Gradient-boosted trees capture that without tuning,
           handle missing values natively, and train in seconds on about 
          2,500 rows. No search is performed (non-negotiable). |
      68  | D8 | Target: log-change `y_h = log1p(Q[d0+h]) − log1p(Q[d0])`
          . | Absolute `log1p(Q)`; raw cfs. | AI | Tree models cannot pre
          dict beyond the target range seen in training. Predicting a rat
          io relative to today's flow lets forecasts scale with the curre
          nt state (RK5). `log1p` handles near-zero flow (RK10). The pers
          istence forecast corresponds to `y_h = 0`, which makes model-vs
          -baseline comparison natural. |
      69  | D9 | Intervals: per horizon, calibration residuals `r = y_tru
          e − y_pred` in log-change space. Store the quantiles at 0.025, 
          0.10, 0.50, 0.90, 0.975. Serve `q_p = log1p(Q[d0]) + ŷ + r_p`, 
          then `cfs = max(0, expm1(q_p))`. The median uses `r_0.50` (bias
           correction). Models are **not** refit on calibration data. | Q
          uantile regression models; conformal wrappers; residuals in cfs
          . | AI | This is exactly what the brief asks for. Log space giv
          es multiplicative bands, which fit flow errors that grow with f
          low. Using monotone transforms of ordered quantiles guarantees 
          band ordering. Not refitting keeps the calibration residuals ho
          nest out-of-sample. |

      74  | D14 | Value validity: **physically impossible** = non-numeric
          , NaN, negative, or the USGS sentinel `-999999` → dropped and c
          ounted in the log. **Extreme but real** = any finite value ≥ 0 
          → always kept, never clipped or winsorized. `ESTIMATED` qualifi
          ers and `Provisional` status are kept and flagged. | Outlier re
          moval by z-score or IQR. | AI | Floods are the signal this fore
          cast exists for. The legacy `noDataValue` observed was `-999999
          .0`. The new API showed no sentinel, but the check is kept defe
          nsively. |
      75  | D15 | Two Compose services built from the same image: `pipeli
          ne` (scheduler) and `dashboard` (Streamlit), sharing the named 
          volume `sfdata` mounted at `/data`. | A single container runnin
          g both processes. | AI | Two processes with different lifecycle
          s (a batch loop vs a web server) are what Compose is for. A sin
          gle container would need a process supervisor. One image keeps 
          the build single. |
      76  | D16 | Dashboard charts use Altair (already a Streamlit depend

      134  - **USGS (new API):** `features[].properties` has `time` (YYYY
           -MM-DD, local date), `value` (**string**), `unit_of_measure` (
           `ft^3/s`), `approval_status` (`Approved` / `Provisional`), `qu
           alifier` (null or a list, e.g. `["ESTIMATED"]`), `statistic_id
           `, `last_modified`, `monitoring_location_id` (`USGS-02085000`)
           . Rows come back **unordered**. Without `statistic_id=00003` t
           he response mixes min/max/mean. Counts for 2006-01-01..2026-09
           -22: 7,568 rows over 7,570 days (real gaps exist); Approved 7,
           527, Provisional 41; qualifier null 7,441, `["ESTIMATED"]` 127
           ; min 0.22 cfs, max 8,180 cfs on 2025-07-07; no zeros or negat
           ives. The newest value was 2026-09-22 (today not yet posted).
      135  - **Open-Meteo forecast:** `https://api.open-meteo.com/v1/fore
           cast?latitude=36.0711&longitude=-79.0956&daily=precipitation_s
           um,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min&forecast_days=3&past_
           days=7&timezone=America%2FNew_York`. Response `daily.time`, `d
           aily.precipitation_sum` (mm), `daily.temperature_2m_max/min` (
           °C), `daily_units`, `utc_offset_seconds` (-14400 in EDT). **Fo
           recast day 1 is today.** It snaps to the grid cell (36.072, -7
           9.109).
      136 -- **Open-Meteo historical-forecast:** `https://historical-fore
          -cast-api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast` with the same daily varia
          -bles plus `start_date` and `end_date`. It accepts dates from 2
          -016-01-01 (an earlier `start_date` returns `{"error":true,"rea
          -son":"Parameter 'start_date' is out of allowed range from 2016
          --01-01 to ..."}`). Same grid cell and values as the forecast e
          -ndpoint's `past_days`.

          -he run.

          +modifyitems` hook in `tests/conftest.py` raises a collection e
          +rror for any test that doesn't carry exactly one of the three

● Update(docs\plan.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      305  - [ ] AC-2.3 Extreme but real values are kept unchanged: using
            the Chantal fixture, the 2025-07-07 value 8,180 cfs appears i
           n the output `flow_cfs` exactly. No clipping, winsorizing, or 
           outlier filtering exists in the module.
      306  - [ ] AC-2.4 Gaps: flow gaps of at most `MAX_FFILL_DAYS` are f
           orward-filled for lag inputs only (flag column `flow_filled`).
            Longer gaps leave NaN, and rows whose lags or target are NaN 
           are excluded from training rows. Targets are never filled.
      307  - [ ] AC-2.5 Date alignment: a rain day and the flow on the sa
           me America/New_York date land on the same row. With fixture da
           ta where rain on day D is followed by a rise on D or D+1, `pre
           cip_d0` on row D equals that day's rain.
      308 -- [ ] AC-2.6 No leakage: for any issue date d0, changing any f
          -low value after d0 or any weather value after d0+3 leaves ever
          -y feature on row d0 unchanged (property test on perturbed copi
          -es).                                                          
      308 +- [ ] AC-2.6 No leakage: for any issue date d0, changing any f
          +low value after d0 leaves every feature column on row d0 uncha
          +nged (targets `y_1..y_3` excluded, since they are future flow 
          +by definition). Changing any weather value after d0 changes on
          +ly `precip_f1..f3` (the documented exception in R6 and RK3), a
          +nd changing weather after d0+3 changes nothing. Tested as a pr
          +operty test on perturbed copies.                              
      309  - [ ] AC-2.7 Output `data/features/features_<ts>.csv` with col
           umns: `date` (d0), `flow_cfs`, `approval_status`, `flow_filled
           `, `logq_0`, `logq_1`, `logq_2`, `dlogq_1`, `precip_0`, `api_7
           `, `api_30`, `precip_f1`, `precip_f2`, `precip_f3`, `tmax_0`, 
           `tmin_0`, `doy_sin`, `doy_cos`, `y_1`, `y_2`, `y_3`. It includ

           res files are kept.

            year of d0; `y_h = log1p(flow[d0+h]) − logq_0`.

● I've written docs/plan.md (Project section plus Stages 0–6, each with numbered
  acceptance criteria, tests, and a smoke test) and made no other changes to
  the repo.

  One change you need to confirm: training now starts in 2018, not 2016. The
  historical-forecast weather source accepts a 2016 start date but returns empty
  values for every day of 2016 and 2017; other weather models are empty for
  2016 too. So I set the start to 2018-01-01 and logged it as D18, an AI
  decision that amends your D3. Training becomes 2018–2022, about 1,800 days per
  horizon. That period still has 36 of the 76 days at or above the 99th
  percentile (653 cfs), with a peak of 2,760 cfs. It loses the 2017 floods
  (3,270 and 2,290 cfs) and Matthew (2016). If you'd rather fill 2016–2017 from
  the other weather source, tell me and I'll change it.

  The 5 decisions that most shape the build
  1. Weather (D2, yours): train on the historical-forecast endpoint, the same
     model and grid the live forecast uses. This replaces the brief's
     "historical archive". The cost is one documented exception to the
     no-future-information rule: in training, the forecast-rain features are
     slightly better than a real 1–3-day forecast, so the t+3 bands will likely
     be too narrow.
  2. Splits (D3 and D18): train 2018–2022, calibration 2023–2024, test 2025
     onward. Chantal falls in the test period.
  3. Forecast anchor (D4, yours): forecasts start from the last day USGS has
     posted (d0), normally yesterday. So t+1 is today, and the forecast reports
     how many days stale the data are.
  4. Model (D7 and D8, mine): one gradient-boosted tree model per horizon
     (scikit-learn HistGradientBoostingRegressor, default settings, fixed seed).
     It predicts the change in log flow from today's flow, so floods bigger
     than anything in training aren't capped.
  5. Bands (D9, mine): built from the calibration-period errors in log space.
     The median is bias-corrected, the models are not refit on calibration data,
     and bounds are ordered and never negative. The metrics report coverage for
     flood days separately.

  I also fixed a few gaps before finishing:
  - The dashboard smoke test now uses make dashboard instead of calling
    Streamlit directly.
  - A test hook now fails any test without a unit, regression, or integration
    marker.
  - Every "today" is computed in America/New_York time, since containers run in
    UTC.
  - make pipeline is now assigned to a stage (AC-6.9).

  Still open
  - You need to confirm the 2018 start (D18).
  - The name of the USGS API-key header isn't verified; the Builder checks it.
  - Test fixtures aren't recorded yet. That's the Builder's job, including
    finding a real window with an estimated value next to a gap.
  - There's no git repo yet, so git init is needed before the Gate 1 commit.
  - The skill file is at skills/dev-cycle.md, but the course board expects
    .claude/skills/dev-cycle/SKILL.md.

  Gate 1, reading the plan end to end and pushing back, is yours. Use the review
  prompts or send your own edits and I'll log them as Student decisions.

● The bands come from the model's errors on 2023–2024, measured in log space and
  added to each new forecast. A new make coverage check (AC-3.9) measures on
  2025 onward whether the 80% band actually holds about 80% of outcomes.

  How the intervals are computed

  1. Where the errors come from: the calibration period, 2023-01-01 to
  2024-12-31.
  - There is one model per horizon (h = 1, 2, 3), each trained only on
    2018–2022.
  - Each model predicts every calibration day. A day counts only if both its
    issue date d0 and its target date d0+h fall inside 2023–2024, so nothing
    leaks across the split edge.
  - The model never saw these days, so the errors are honest out-of-sample
    errors. We don't refit on 2023–2024 afterwards; refitting would make them
    in-sample errors.
  - That gives about 730 errors per horizon.

  2. What space: the log of flow, not cfs.
  - The model predicts the log-change y = log1p(Q[d0+h]) − log1p(Q[d0]).
  - Each error is r = y_true − y_pred, in that same log-change space.
  - We store five quantiles of r per horizon: 2.5%, 10%, 50%, 90% and 97.5%.
  - Why log space: flow errors grow with flow. Being off by 5 cfs matters at 10
    cfs, not at 2,000. Log errors are roughly the same size across flow levels,
    so one set of quantiles works in drought and in flood. Once converted back
    to cfs, the bands are multiplicative: wide when the river is high, narrow
    when it's low.

  3. How they're applied to a new forecast. For horizon h with prediction ŷ and
  today's logq_0 = log1p(Q[d0]):
  q_p   = logq_0 + ŷ + r_p            for p ∈ {0.025, 0.10, 0.50, 0.90, 0.975}
  cfs_p = max(0, expm1(q_p))
  median = cfs_0.50   lo80/hi80 = cfs_0.10/cfs_0.90   lo95/hi95 =
  cfs_0.025/cfs_0.975
  - The median includes r_0.50, which corrects any systematic bias the model
    shows on calibration data.
  - Converting back to cfs never changes the order of values, so the bounds
    always come out as lo95 ≤ lo80 ≤ median ≤ hi80 ≤ hi95.
  - max(0, …) guards against negative cfs.

  Illustrative numbers, not real output: today is 50 cfs, so logq_0 = 3.93. Say
  ŷ = +0.40 for a forecast rise, and the 10%/90% errors are −0.35/+0.30.
  - The 80% band is expm1(3.98) to expm1(4.63), about 53 to 101 cfs.
  - The same log errors at 1,000 cfs give a band about 20 times wider in cfs.

  How we know the 80% band covers about 80%

  We use three checks. Each catches a different failure.

  Check: Calibration coverage within ±0.02 of 80% and 95%
  Data: 2023–2024
  What a failure means: The quantiles are computed from this same data, so it
  should match almost exactly. If it doesn't, the bands are applied wrong:
  wrong sign, wrong space, or wrong column.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: Test coverage in [0.72, 0.88] for the 80% band and [0.90, 0.99] for the

  95% band, every horizon
  Data: 2025 onward (~630 days, includes Chantal)
  What a failure means: This is the honest number. The bands miss too often or
  are too wide on data they've never seen.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Check: High-flow coverage (flow above the 2018–2022 90th percentile), reported

  but not gated
  Data: 2025 onward
  What a failure means: Shows whether the bands hold up during floods, where
  they
  matter most and where we expect them to be too narrow.

  Why the tolerance is ±0.08 and not ±0.03 (logged as D19). A simple binomial
  calculation with n ≈ 630 would give about ±0.03. But daily errors come in
  runs: one wet week the model underestimates produces several misses in a row.
  The effective number of independent outcomes is closer to about 150, and at
  that size 2.5 standard errors is about ±0.08. The 95% band's upper bound is
  capped at 0.99, because bands that almost never miss are too wide to be
  useful.

  Two safeguards keep the number honest:
  - Coverage is computed in cfs with the same band function Forecast uses, and a
    regression test proves both give identical bounds. The measured coverage
    therefore describes the bands users actually see.
  - If make coverage fails on real data, that's a finding for you to triage. It
    must not be "fixed" by widening the tolerances or refitting the quantiles on
    the test period.

  My prediction: the 2025-onward check may fail at t+3. Training uses
  historical-forecast rain, which is nearly observed rain (RK3), and 2023–2024
  had few floods (RK6). Both push toward bands that are too narrow. The new
  check is designed to show that failure rather than hide it.

  Changes to docs/plan.md

  - New AC-3.9: a make coverage table that fails if calibration coverage isn't
    within ±0.02 or test coverage is outside the D19 tolerances, for any
    horizon. Coverage bounds are inclusive and computed in cfs; high-flow
    coverage is reported only.
  - AC-3.8 now warns on the same condition, so the warning and the make coverage
    check can't disagree.
  - New decision D19: the tolerances and why.
  - New tests: test_coverage_report_thresholds and
    test_coverage_inclusive_and_in_cfs (Stage 3), and
    test_forecast_bands_match_train_bands (Stage 4).
  - make coverage added to the targets table, and to the Stage 3 smoke test with
    what to watch for.

  Handoff

  Role · Stage: Architect · Gate 1 revisions
  Changed: docs/plan.md (AC-3.8 reworded; AC-3.9, D19, the make coverage target,
  three tests, and a smoke-test step added)
  Evidence: plan edits only; no code, nothing run
  Status: acceptance criteria now include AC-3.9
  Needs a decision from you: accept the D19 tolerances or set your own (I'll log
  yours as Student); D18 (start in 2018) is still waiting for your confirmation
  Next step: continue Gate 1 with the next review prompt

● One correction first: after D2 we don't train on observed weather. We train on
  Open-Meteo's historical-forecast data. But that data is built from forecasts
  made about 0 days ahead, so it's close to observed, and the problem you
  describe still applies. I've rewritten risk RK3 in docs/plan.md to answer your
  question.

  How it biases the intervals

  Live forecast rain is much worse than the training rain. At serve time,
  precip_f1 is a same-day forecast, but precip_f2 and precip_f3 are forecasts
  made 1 and 2 days ahead. I pulled the lead-specific archive for two recent
  days:

  ┌────────────┬──────────────────────────┬──────────┬───────────┬─────────┐
  │  Rain on   │ Same-day forecast (what  │  1 day   │  2 days   │  Gauge  │
  │            │       we train on)       │  ahead   │   ahead   │         │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────┼───────────┼─────────┤
  │ 2026-09-18 │ 51.5 mm (all in one      │ 0.7 mm   │ 4.1 mm    │ flat    │
  │            │ hour)                    │          │           │         │
  ├────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────┼───────────┼─────────┤
  │ 2026-09-21 │ 47.2 mm                  │ 0.0 mm   │ 24.5 mm   │ small   │
  │            │                          │          │           │ rise    │
  └────────────┴──────────────────────────┴──────────┴───────────┴─────────┘

  The effects:
  1. Bands too narrow. The calibration errors never include the error from
     forecasting rain 1–2 days ahead, so the 80% and 95% bands under-cover.
  2. Worse with horizon. t+1 is barely affected; t+3 is affected most.
  3. The misses land on rain days. A missed storm puts the outcome above the
     band; a false alarm puts it below. These are the same flood days where
     2023–2024 calibration is already weak (RK6), so the two problems stack.
     Dry-weather recession days are fine.
  4. The median over-reacts to forecast rain. The model learned to trust rain
     that was nearly certain. The median bias correction can't fix that, because
     it is also measured with same-day rain.
  5. My own AC-3.9 can mislead. The 2025-onward test also uses same-day rain, so
     make coverage could pass while live forecasts under-cover. This is the
     most important issue.

  Fix or document? Fix most of it, document the rest

  Open-Meteo has a previous-runs API that archives what each forecast said 1, 2
  and 3 days ahead. I verified it covers this site from 2024-01-19 onward, at
  the same grid cell. During Chantal (2025-07-06) it shows 87.8 mm forecast 1
  day ahead and 5.8 mm 2 days ahead.

  Proposed D20:
  - Training stays on historical-forecast. No lead-specific archive exists
    before 2024.
  - Calibration and test rows use lead-matched rain. Today's rain comes from the
    same-day forecast; tomorrow's and the next day's come from the forecasts
    issued 1 and 2 days earlier.
  - Result: the calibration errors and the median correction then include the
    real forecast-rain error, and make coverage measures the bands users will
    actually see.
  - Cost: one more ingest source, which is hourly only, so it must be summed per
    local date. The splits also change:

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │             │ Now (D3/D18)  │                 With D20                  │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Train       │ 2018–2022     │ 2018–2023                                 │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ 2023–2024     │ 2024-02-01..2024-12-31 (~330 days,        │
  │ Calibration │ (~730 days)   │ includes Debby and Helene at about 1,800  │
  │             │               │ cfs)                                      │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Test        │ 2025+         │ 2025+ (fully lead-matched)                │
  └─────────────┴───────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┘

  What stays a documented limitation either way: the model trains on
  near-perfect rain, so it over-trusts forecast rain and its point skill is
  lower than a lead-matched model's would be. There's no archive to fix that
  before 2024.

  If you decline D20: the plan keeps effects 1–5 as a documented limitation. It
  also relabels AC-3.9 as an upper bound on live coverage, with the same caveat
  on the dashboard's coverage number.

  My recommendation is to accept D20. Without it, the coverage check gives false
  comfort. The cost is one extra data source and a smaller calibration set. I
  haven't changed the splits or any acceptance criteria yet, because D3 is your
  decision.

          . | AI | This is exactly what the brief asks for. Log space giv

      140  - **USGS (new API):** `features[].properties` has `time` (YYYY
           -MM-DD, local date), `value` (**string**), `unit_of_measure` (
           `ft^3/s`), `approval_status` (`Approved` / `Provisional`), `qu
           alifier` (null or a list, e.g. `["ESTIMATED"]`), `statistic_id
           `, `last_modified`, `monitoring_location_id` (`USGS-02085000`)
           . Rows come back **unordered**. Without `statistic_id=00003` t
           he response mixes min/max/mean. Counts for 2006-01-01..2026-09
           -22: 7,568 rows over 7,570 days (real gaps exist); Approved 7,
           527, Provisional 41; qualifier null 7,441, `["ESTIMATED"]` 127
           ; min 0.22 cfs, max 8,180 cfs on 2025-07-07; no zeros or negat
           ives. The newest value was 2026-09-22 (today not yet posted).
      141  - **Open-Meteo forecast:** `https://api.open-meteo.com/v1/fore
           cast?latitude=36.0711&longitude=-79.0956&daily=precipitation_s
           um,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min&forecast_days=3&past_
           days=7&timezone=America%2FNew_York`. Response `daily.time`, `d
           aily.precipitation_sum` (mm), `daily.temperature_2m_max/min` (
           °C), `daily_units`, `utc_offset_seconds` (-14400 in EDT). **Fo
           recast day 1 is today.** It snaps to the grid cell (36.072, -7
           9.109).
      142  - **Open-Meteo historical-forecast:** `https://historical-fore
           cast-api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast` with the same daily varia
           bles plus `start_date` and `end_date`. It accepts dates from 2
           016-01-01 (an earlier `start_date` returns `{"error":true,"rea
           son":"Parameter 'start_date' is out of allowed range from 2016
           -01-01 to ..."}`), **but all values are null until 2017-12-31*
           * (RK17). One request for 2016-01-01..2026-09-22 returned 3,91
           8 days (109 KB) in a single response, so no chunking is needed
           . Same grid cell and values as the forecast endpoint's `past_d
           ays`.
      143 -- **Open-Meteo previous-runs (proposed in D20):** `https://pre
          -vious-runs-api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast?...&hourly=precipita
          -tion,precipitation_previous_day1,precipitation_previous_day2&t
          -imezone=America%2FNew_York`. **Hourly only**: `daily=precipita
          -tion_sum_previous_day1` returns `{"error":true,"reason":"Inval
          -id value: ... precipitation_sum_previous_day1"}`. Same grid ce
          -ll as the forecast endpoint. The first non-null values are `pr
          -evious_day1` 2024-01-19T08:00, `previous_day2` 2024-01-20T08:0
          -0, `previous_day3` 2024-01-21T08:00, so all of 2024 from Febru
          -ary onward and all of 2025–2026 are covered. On 2025-07-06 (Ch
          -antal), `previous_day1` = 87.8 mm and `previous_day2` = 5.8 mm
          -.                                                             
      143 +- **Open-Meteo previous-runs (adopted in D20):** `https://prev
          +ious-runs-api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast?...&hourly=precipitat
          +ion,precipitation_previous_day1,precipitation_previous_day2&ti
          +mezone=America%2FNew_York`. **Hourly only**: `daily=precipitat
          +ion_sum_previous_day1` returns `{"error":true,"reason":"Invali
          +d value: ... precipitation_sum_previous_day1"}`. Same grid cel
          +l as the forecast endpoint. The first non-null values are `pre
          +vious_day1` 2024-01-19T08:00, `previous_day2` 2024-01-20T08:00
          +, `previous_day3` 2024-01-21T08:00, so all of 2024 from Februa
          +ry onward and all of 2025–2026 are covered. On 2025-07-06 (Cha
          +ntal), `previous_day1` = 87.8 mm and `previous_day2` = 5.8 mm.
      144  - **Open-Meteo archive (not used):** the default model fills t
           hrough **today** (a partial day); with `models=era5` it is nul
           l after about 6 days. Different grid cells again. This is why 
           D2 and RK9 exist.

      457 -- [ ] AC-4.5 Staleness: `stale_days = (paths.local_today() − 1
          - day) − d0` (America/New_York, R14). If `stale_days > 0`, fore
          -cast from d0 anyway and log a WARNING. If `stale_days > MAX_ST
          -ALE_DAYS`, log an ERROR, write nothing, and exit non-zero.    
      458 -- [ ] AC-4.6 A corrupt or unloadable checkpoint (bad bytes, mi
          -ssing keys, `sklearn_version` mismatch): log an ERROR naming t
          -he file, write nothing, exit non-zero.                        
      459 -- [ ] AC-4.7 Fewer than 3 forecast days (any of `precip_f1..f3
          -` missing on the latest row): log an ERROR `forecast weather h
          -as N of 3 days`, write nothing, exit non-zero.                
      460 -- [ ] AC-4.8 A provisional latest observation is used as-is an
          -d flagged with `latest_obs_provisional = True`.               
      457 +- [ ] AC-4.5 **USGS hasn't posted yesterday.** `stale_days = (
          +paths.local_today() − 1 day) − d0` (America/New_York, R14).   
      458 +  - `stale_days = 0`: normal.                                 
      459 +  - `1 ≤ stale_days ≤ MAX_STALE_DAYS` (2): forecast from d0 an
          +yway. Valid dates stay d0+1..d0+3, so one or two of them may a
          +lready be in the past. Log a WARNING `USGS data through <d0>, 
          +<k> day(s) stale`. `stale_days` is written on every row.      
      460 +  - `stale_days > MAX_STALE_DAYS`: log an ERROR `USGS data thr
          +ough <d0> is <k> days stale (limit 2); not forecasting`, write
          + nothing, exit 1.                                             
      461 +  - Never fill the missing days (no persistence fill, no extra
          +polation).                                                    
      462 +- [ ] AC-4.6 A corrupt or unloadable checkpoint (bad bytes, mi
          +ssing keys, `sklearn_version` mismatch): log an ERROR naming t
          +he file, write nothing, exit 1.                               
      463 +- [ ] AC-4.7 **Fewer than 3 forecast days.** If any of `precip
          +_f1..f3` on the latest row is missing or null (a truncated or 
          +partly-null Open-Meteo response): log an ERROR `forecast weath
          +er has N of 3 days for <d0+1>..<d0+3>; not forecasting`, write
          + nothing, exit 1. No partial files with 1 or 2 rows are ever w
          +ritten.                                                       
      464 +- [ ] AC-4.8 **Provisional or estimated latest value.** It is 
          +used as-is (no refusal, no band widening), and flagged with `l
          +atest_obs_provisional` (`approval_status == "Provisional"`) an
          +d `latest_obs_estimated` (`qualifier` contains `ESTIMATED`). L
          +ogged at INFO.                                                
      465 +- [ ] AC-4.9 **Incomplete flow history.** If any of `logq_0`, 
          +`logq_1`, `logq_2` on the latest row is NaN after the Features
          + forward-fill (a gap longer than `MAX_FFILL_DAYS` within d0−2.
          +.d0): log an ERROR `flow lags incomplete at <d0>`, write nothi
          +ng, exit 1.                                                   
      466 +- [ ] AC-4.10 **Rate limit or outage upstream.** Forecast make
          +s no HTTP calls; ingest handles retries (AC-1.5, AC-1.6). Fore
          +cast requires `fc_fetched_date == paths.local_today()` on the 
          +latest row. If the newest forecast weather is older (for examp
          +le because today's Open-Meteo call hit 429 on every retry), lo
          +g an ERROR `forecast weather is stale (fetched <date>); not fo
          +recasting`, write nothing, exit 1. A USGS-only failure is hand
          +led by the staleness rules in AC-4.5.                         
      467 +- [ ] AC-4.11 **Refusals are safe.** In every refusal case (AC
          +-4.5 beyond the limit, 4.6, 4.7, 4.9, 4.10), earlier `forecast
          +_*.csv` files are left untouched, and the scheduler continues 
          +its loop. Exit codes: 0 = forecast written, or no checkpoint y
          +et (AC-4.4); 1 = refused or checkpoint unusable.

● I've added the missing-data rules to docs/plan.md and applied D19 and D20
  through the plan, both logged as your decisions.

  What Forecast does in each case (new decision D21)

  Case: USGS hasn't posted yesterday
  What happens: It forecasts from the last posted day if that day is at most 2
  days behind, with a warning; stale_days goes on every row. If the data are 3
  or more days behind, it refuses. It never fills in missing days.
  Criterion: AC-4.5
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Latest value is provisional or estimated
  What happens: It uses the value as-is and marks it provisional or estimated.
  The dashboard shows a note.
  Criterion: AC-4.8
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Open-Meteo returns fewer than 3 forecast days (or empty values)
  What happens: Ingest saves what came back and logs a warning. Forecast
  refuses,
  and never writes a file with 1 or 2 rows.
  Criterion: AC-1.4, AC-4.7
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Rate limit (429) or outage
  What happens: Ingest retries 3 times, waits as long as the server's
  Retry-After
  header asks (up to 60 s), then fails that one source only. Forecast then
  refuses if today's weather forecast wasn't fetched today.
  Criterion: AC-1.5, AC-1.6, AC-4.10
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Added: gap in recent flow longer than the 2-day forward-fill
  What happens: It refuses.
  Criterion: AC-4.9
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Any refusal
  What happens: Earlier forecast files are left untouched and the scheduler
  keeps
  running. Exit code 0 means a forecast was written or no model exists yet; 1
  means it refused.
  Criterion: AC-4.11

  The dashboard also shows an "Forecast outdated — last issued …" banner, a
  stale-data banner, and greys out forecast points whose dates are already past
  (AC-5.5).

  Why these choices:
  - Refuse rather than invent inputs. A forecast built from made-up or old data
    looks just as confident as a real one.
  - Don't refuse on provisional data. Every USGS value from 2026 is still
    provisional (41 of 41), so refusing would block almost every run.
  - Two days is the stale limit. At 3 days behind, all three forecast dates are
    already in the past; at 2, today still gets a forecast.

  How D20 changes the rest of the plan

  - New splits: train 2018-01-01 to 2023-12-31 (about 2,190 rows), calibration
    2024-02-01 to 2024-12-31 (335 rows; includes Debby and Helene), test 2025
    onward. January 2024 is unused. New settings CALIBRATION_START and
    LEADS_START; TRAIN_END is now 2023-12-31.
  - Ingest gets a fourth source, the previous-runs forecast archive. It is
    hourly only, so ingest sums hours per local date and treats a day with any
    missing hour as missing, including the 23- and 25-hour days when clocks
    change (AC-1.9).
  - Features: calibration and test days use the forecast as it stood 1 and 2
    days ahead, and are never back-filled with the near-observed rain (AC-2.8).
    A test uses 2026-09-18 to check that the 1-day-ahead value (0.7 mm) is used,
    not 51.5 mm.
  - Train stops with an error if any calibration or test day lacks the matched
    forecast (AC-3.1).
  - Flood risk (RK6) is updated: calibration now has only 6 days at or above 653
    cfs, and each 95% band edge rests on about 8 errors.

  D18 (training starts in 2018) still needs your confirmation. The new splits
  assume it.

  Handoff

  Role · Stage: Architect · Gate 1 revisions
  Changed: docs/plan.md
  - New decisions: D20 and D21. D3 and D18 now carry amendment notes; D19 is
    marked Student.
  - Settings: TRAIN_END, CALIBRATION_START, LEADS_START, and MAX_STALE_DAYS
    (now 2).
  - Risks updated: R3, R6, RK3, RK6, RK8, RK12.
  - Criteria: new AC-1.9, 2.8, 4.9–4.11; revised AC-0.4, 1.1, 1.2, 1.4, 1.5,
    1.7, 2.6, 2.7, 3.1, 3.2, 4.1, 4.5, 4.7, 4.8, 5.5; tests and smoke tests
    updated for Stages 1–5.

  Evidence: plan edits only. The new flood counts come from the saved USGS data:
  2024-02-01 to 2024-12-31 has 335 days, 6 of them at or above 653 cfs; the
  training 90th percentile is 152 cfs.

  Needs a decision from you: confirm D18.

● No. With only 1 or 2 days of forecast rain, Forecast writes nothing for that
  run: no rows at all (AC-4.7). The dashboard keeps showing the previous
  forecast with the "Forecast outdated" banner, and the next run tries again.

  The reason is the current design (AC-3.2): all three models take all three
  forecast-rain values as inputs. The t+1 model also receives the rain for days
  2 and 3, so if day 3 is missing, no horizon can run.

  Alternative, if you'd rather get partial forecasts: give each model only the
  rain up to its own target day.

  ┌─────────────┬───────────┬───────────┬───────────┐
  │             │ t+1 model │ t+2 model │ t+3 model │
  ├─────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┤
  │ Rain inputs │ day 1     │ days 1–2  │ days 1–3  │
  └─────────────┴───────────┴───────────┴───────────┘

  - Gain: with 2 days of rain you'd get t+1 and t+2 rows; with 1 day, only t+1.
    It's also more physically sensible, since rain after the target day can't
    affect flow on that day, and it's one less input for the model to learn to
    ignore.
  - Cost:
    - AC-4.1 changes from exactly 3 rows to 1–3 rows.
    - The dashboard fan chart and headline have to handle a shorter forecast.
    - There are more test cases.

  My recommendation: keep refusing. Open-Meteo returning fewer than 3 days is
  rare (I never saw it), the dashboard already covers the gap, and
  exactly-3-rows keeps every downstream check simple. Per-horizon rain inputs
  are a reasonable change on their own merits, though. If you want either one,
  say so and I'll log it as your decision.

● Final plan

  1. Goal: every day, forecast the Eno River's daily mean flow at USGS gauge
     02085000 for 1, 2 and 3 days ahead, with a median, 80% and 95% bands, and
     the "no change" persistence baseline alongside. It's shown in a Streamlit
     dashboard.
  2. Data:
     - USGS through the new api.waterdata.usgs.gov API. The legacy service shuts
       down in early 2027 (D1).
     - Open-Meteo historical-forecast weather for training, and the live
       forecast endpoint (plus 7 past days) at run time (D2).
     - Open-Meteo previous-runs archive for 1- and 2-day-ahead rain (D20).
     - All dates are America/New_York days, and "today" is always computed in
       that timezone.
  3. Pipeline: ingest, features, train, forecast and dashboard hand off only
     through files under DATA_DIR. A test checks that no stage imports another
     (D11).
  4. Splits:
     - train 2018-01-01 to 2023-12-31;
     - calibration 2024-02-01 to 2024-12-31;
     - test 2025 onward, which includes Chantal.
       Splits are by date and never shuffled. Calibration and test days use rain
       as it was forecast 1 and 2 days ahead (D3, D18, D20).
  5. Model: one scikit-learn HistGradientBoostingRegressor per horizon with
     default settings and a fixed seed. It predicts the change in log flow from
     today's flow, so floods beyond the training range aren't capped (D7, D8).
  6. Bands: calibration-period errors in log space, stored as percentiles and
     added to each new prediction. The median is bias-corrected, bounds never go
     below zero, and the ordering lo95 ≤ lo80 ≤ median ≤ hi80 ≤ hi95 always
     holds (D9).
  7. Verification:
     - The metrics file reports skill against persistence and band coverage,
       overall and for high-flow days.
     - make coverage fails if 2025-onward coverage falls outside [0.72, 0.88]
       for the 80% band or [0.90, 0.99] for the 95% band, or if calibration
       coverage is off by more than ±0.02 (D19).
  8. Data quality: impossible values are dropped; floods are always kept. Flow
     gaps of up to 2 days are forward-filled and never interpolated. Forecast
     refuses on data more than 2 days stale, fewer than 3 forecast days, weather
     not fetched today, gaps in recent flow, or an unusable model. A refusal
     writes no partial rows and leaves earlier forecasts untouched, and the
     dashboard marks the last forecast as outdated (D13, D14, D21, D22).
  9. Operations:
     - The Makefile is the only interface.
     - Automated tests never reach the network (recorded responses, mocked HTTP,
       network calls blocked), and CI runs Python 3.12 on GitHub Actions.
     - Docker Compose runs two services from one non-root image: a scheduler
       (daily pipeline, weekly retrain) and the dashboard with a healthcheck,
       sharing the named volume sfdata (D5, D6, D15).
  10. Known limitations:
      - The model is trained on nearly-observed rain, so it over-trusts forecast
        rain (RK3d).
      - Calibration has few flood days (RK6).
      - The 2025-onward coverage check may fail on real data. If it does, that's
        a finding to triage, not something to tune away.

> If the model performs poorly with the default parameters, is there a plan to
  try to improve it by testing a different ML method or tweaking parameters? I
  moved the skill and ran git init

● Checking that the skill move and the git setup look right.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● No. The plan deliberately doesn't try another model or tune parameters. The
  brief rules it out: "No model comparison, no hyperparameter search." Here is
  what happens instead if the model does poorly:

  1. Training detects it. If the model doesn't beat persistence at any horizon,
     or coverage falls outside the D19 range, training logs a warning (AC-3.8)
     and make coverage fails (AC-3.9).
  2. The Tester reports it as a finding with the metrics as evidence.
  3. You decide at Gate 3: fix it, or accept it and document why.

  Good fixes to try first (not tuning): poor skill is more often a bug than the
  wrong model. Candidates include:
  - rain and flow joined on the wrong date;
  - a sign error in the log-change target or the bands;
  - lead-matched rain missing on too many days;
  - features that don't match what the model was trained on.

  Fixing any of these fixes the implementation to match the plan.

  If the pipeline is correct and the model still loses to persistence, you could
  make one justified change as a Student decision, for example Ridge regression
  instead of gradient boosting, or per-horizon rain inputs (the D22 option).
  Two rules keep that honest:
  - Never judge the change on the 2025-onward test data. Choosing a model
    because it scored better there means the test number no longer measures
    anything.
  - Don't judge it on the 2024 calibration data either, because that data sets
    the bands. Use 2023, the last training year, held out.

  Also, expect t+1 to be hard to beat. On most dry days flow barely changes, so
  "no change" is nearly perfect. Skill near zero at t+1 but positive on rain
  days or at t+3 would be a real, honest result, not a failure. The sidecar
  could split skill into rain days and dry days; say so if you want that added.

  I'd add this as a short pre-committed protocol in the Decisions log (D23).
  Deciding the rules before seeing the results is what makes a later change
  defensible. It would say:
  - diagnose first;
  - then at most one change, decided by you;
  - judged on 2023 only;
  - the test period is never used for choosing.

  Should I add it?

  and docs/ untracked). But the skill landed at .claude/skills/dev-cycle.md.

  board's path is .claude/skills/dev-cycle/SKILL.md. Move and rename it:

  .claude/skills/dev-cycle.md .claude/skills/dev-cycle/SKILL.md

  reviewed".

● Update(docs\plan.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      238  - [ ] AC-1.5 All HTTP goes through one function with a timeout
           , `HTTP_RETRIES` attempts, and exponential backoff on 429/5xx/
           connection errors. A `Retry-After` header is honoured, capped 
           at 60 s.
      239  - [ ] AC-1.6 On final failure (HTTP error, Open-Meteo `{"error
           ": true}` body, or malformed JSON), ingest logs an ERROR namin
           g the source and reason, writes no partial file for that sourc
           e, still attempts the other sources, and exits non-zero. An em
           pty USGS response (`features: []`) is logged as WARNING, write
           s no file, and is not a crash.
      240  - [ ] AC-1.7 `INGEST_SOURCE=fixtures make ingest` produces the
            same four file types from `tests/fixtures/` with no network a
           ccess.
      241 +- [ ] AC-1.10 **Timezone pinning.** Every Open-Meteo request (
          +forecast, historical-forecast, previous-runs) includes `timezo
          +ne=America/New_York`, taken from `TIMEZONE`. Ingest checks tha
          +t the response's `timezone` field equals the requested value; 
          +on a mismatch it logs an ERROR `timezone mismatch: requested A
          +merica/New_York, got <tz>` and writes no file for that source.
          + USGS `time` and Open-Meteo `daily.time` are written verbatim 
          +as `YYYY-MM-DD` strings: never parsed as timestamps, never loc
          +alized, never converted to UTC.                               
      242  - [ ] AC-1.9 Previous-runs CSV `raw/weather_leads/weather_lead
           s_<ts>.csv` has columns `date, lead_days, precip_mm, n_hours, 
           fetched_at_utc`, with `lead_days ∈ {1, 2}`, from hourly `preci
           pitation_previous_day1` and `precipitation_previous_day2` requ
           ested with `timezone=America/New_York`. `precip_mm` is the sum
            of the hourly values on that local date. It is written only w
           hen every hour of that date is present and non-null (`n_hours`
            equals 23, 24, or 25 on DST-change days, as appropriate); oth
           erwise it is NaN, and a WARNING counts the incomplete days.
      243  - [ ] AC-1.8 Files are written atomically (write to `.tmp`, th
           en rename), so a crash never leaves a half-written CSV that ma
           tches the pattern.

           8 days (109 KB) in a single response, so no chunking is needed
           . Same grid cell and values as the forecast endpoint's `past_d
           ays`.

           , `previous_day3` 2024-01-21T08:00, so all of 2024 from Februa
           ry onward and all of 2025–2026 are covered. On 2025-07-06 (Cha
           ntal), `previous_day1` = 87.8 mm and `previous_day2` = 5.8 mm.

● I added D23 and pinned every data source to America/New_York in docs/plan.md,
  with a test built on Tropical Storm Chantal that catches misaligned days.

  D23: what happens if the model performs poorly

  This protocol is written down before any results exist:
  1. Diagnose first. Check the date joins, the signs of the target and the
     bands, whether calibration and test days have their 1- and 2-day-ahead
     rain, and that features match between training and forecasting. Fixing a
     bug doesn't count as a model change.
  2. At most one alternative, and only if the model still doesn't beat
     persistence. You choose it and it's logged as your decision (for example
     Ridge regression, or the per-horizon rain inputs from D22).
  3. Judge it only on 2023, held out from training. Never on the 2024
     calibration data, which sets the bands, and never on the 2025-onward test
     data.
  4. Re-score once on the test data and report the result, even if it's still
     worse than persistence.

  Timezone pinning

  Why it matters. I requested the same Chantal week in both timezones:

  ┌───────┬────────────────────────┬───────────┬──────────────────┐
  │ Date  │ Rain, America/New_York │ Rain, GMT │    USGS flow     │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ 07-05 │ 0.0 mm                 │ 0.0 mm    │ 15.5 cfs         │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ 07-06 │ 54.7 mm                │ 7.9 mm    │ 1,990 cfs        │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ 07-07 │ 15.1 mm                │ 61.9 mm   │ 8,180 cfs (peak) │
  └───────┴────────────────────────┴───────────┴──────────────────┘

  In GMT, the heavy rain lands the day after the flow jumps, so the model would
  learn that flow rises before rain.

  USGS: time is a plain local date. The site record says EST with daylight
  saving in use. The API docs don't say whether a daily value covers a
  standard-time or clock-time day, but that's at most a 1-hour difference in
  summer, which doesn't matter for daily means. The plan treats time as an
  America/New_York date and never converts it.

  Changes to the plan:
  - New AC-1.10:
    - Every Open-Meteo request sends timezone=America/New_York.
    - Ingest rejects any response whose timezone field is different.
    - All dates are stored as YYYY-MM-DD text and never converted to UTC.
    - Tests cover all three endpoints, reject a GMT response, and check for any
      timezone conversion in ingest and features.
  - AC-1.9 test: rain at 21:00–23:00 local time, which is after midnight UTC,
    must count on the local day.
  - AC-2.5 rewritten to use real data:
    - The Chantal records must give 07-06 = 54.7 mm and 1,990 cfs, with the
      first heavy-rain day on the same date as the first 10× flow jump.
    - A GMT copy of the same data, run past the ingest check, must make that
      assertion fail. That proves the test can detect misalignment.
  - New fixtures: openmeteo_hist_chantal_et.json and
    openmeteo_hist_chantal_gmt.json.
  - RK13 updated with the Chantal evidence.
  - Stage 2 smoke test: grep the 07-05 to 07-07 rows and see rain and flow on
    the same day.

  Not added: the rain-day / dry-day skill split I asked about last turn. You
  only approved D23. Tell me if you want it.

  The skill is still in the wrong place. The file is now
  .claude/skills/dev-cycle.md, but Claude Code expects
  .claude/skills/dev-cycle/SKILL.md (the move command is in my previous
  message). If /dev-cycle already shows up for you, it may be loading from your
  personal skills folder instead.

      16  - **R6** Time-series discipline: chronological splits (train 20
          18-01-01..2023-12-31, calibration 2024-02-01..2024-12-31, test 
          2025-01-01 onward; January 2024 is unused). No feature uses inf
          ormation from after the issue time, with **one deliberate, docu
          mented exception, limited to training rows**: there, `precip_f2
          ` and `precip_f3` come from Open-Meteo historical-forecast data
          , which is stitched from forecasts made about 0 days ahead, so 
          they are better than a real 1- or 2-day-ahead forecast (D2, RK3
          ). **Calibration and test rows use lead-matched forecasts** fro
          m the previous-runs archive (D20), so they contain only what wa
          s forecast at issue time. Flow, antecedent precipitation, and t
          emperature features use nothing after d0.

           orecast (today), `precip_f2` is lead-1, and `precip_f3` is lea
           d-2. Those can be very different: for 2026-09-18 the lead-1 an
           d lead-2 forecasts were 0.7 and 4.1 mm; for 2026-09-21, lead-0
            47.2 vs lead-1 0.0 vs lead-2 24.5 mm. **Effects:** (a) the ca
           libration residuals contain no lead-1/lead-2 weather error, so
            the 80%/95% bands are too narrow; (b) the gap grows with hori
           zon (t+1 almost unaffected, t+3 most affected); (c) the misses
            concentrate on rain days (missed storms land above `hi`, fals
           e alarms below `lo`), the same high-flow days RK6 already weak

           over-reacts to forecast rain, and `r_0.50` measured on lead-0 
           inputs cannot correct that; (e) **AC-3.9 as written measures t

      139  | D21 | Forecast behaviour on missing or stale inputs. **USGS 
           late:** forecast from d0 if `stale_days ≤ MAX_STALE_DAYS` (2),
            else refuse. **Provisional/estimated latest value:** use as-i
           s and flag. **Flow lags incomplete** after the 2-day forward-f
           ill: refuse. **Forecast weather has fewer than 3 days, contain
           s nulls, or was not fetched today:** refuse. **Rate limit or o
           utage:** handled in ingest (retry, then fail that source only)
           ; Forecast then applies the staleness rules to whatever files 
           exist. A refusal never writes partial rows and never deletes a
           n earlier forecast; the dashboard shows the newest existing fo
           recast marked as outdated. Exit codes: 0 = forecast written or
            no checkpoint yet; 1 = refused or checkpoint unusable. | Fill
            yesterday by persistence; forecast fewer rows; widen bands wh
           en stale; refuse on provisional data. | AI | Refusing is safer
            than inventing inputs: a forecast built on filled flow or old
            weather looks as confident as a real one. Refusing on provisi
           onal data would block almost every run, because all recent USG
           S values are provisional (41 of 41 in 2026). A limit of 2 days
            is the largest at which at least one valid date (today) is st
           ill in the future. |

            and writes nothing (AC-4.7 unchanged). Every horizon model ke

      141  | D23 | **Protocol if the model performs poorly**, fixed befor
           e any results are seen. (1) **Diagnose first:** check join dat
           es, the target and band signs, the lead-matched coverage, and 
           that features match between training and forecasting; bugs are
            fixed to match the plan and don't count as model changes. (2)
            If the pipeline is correct and the model still fails AC-3.8 (
           no skill vs persistence) at any horizon, **at most one** alter
           native may be tried, chosen by the student and logged here as 
           a new Student decision (e.g. `Ridge` on the same features, or 
           per-horizon rain inputs from D22). (3) The alternative is judg
           ed **only on 2023** (the last training year, held out and refi
           t afterward), never on the calibration period (it sets the ban
           ds) and **never on the 2025+ test split**. (4) Whatever is cho
           sen is re-scored once on test and reported, including when it 
           is still worse than persistence. No grid or random search. | O
           pen-ended tuning; comparing several model families; selecting 
           on test results. | Student (accepted the AI's recommendation a
           fter asking whether there is a plan to improve a poor model) |
            The brief forbids model comparison and hyperparameter search,
            so the default is to accept and report the result. Diagnosing
            first catches the more likely cause (a bug). Pre-committing t
           he rules and keeping selection off the test split keeps the te
           st score an honest estimate. Near-zero skill at t+1 is plausib
           le, because flow barely changes on dry days. It is a result to
            report, not something to tune away. |

      204  - **Open-Meteo forecast:** `https://api.open-meteo.com/v1/fore
           cast?latitude=36.0711&longitude=-79.0956&daily=precipitation_s
           um,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min&forecast_days=3&past_
           days=7&timezone=America%2FNew_York`. Response `daily.time`, `d
           aily.precipitation_sum` (mm), `daily.temperature_2m_max/min` (
           °C), `daily_units`, `utc_offset_seconds` (-14400 in EDT). **Fo
           recast day 1 is today.** It snaps to the grid cell (36.072, -7
           9.109).
      205  - **Open-Meteo historical-forecast:** `https://historical-fore
           cast-api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast` with the same daily varia
           bles plus `start_date` and `end_date`. It accepts dates from 2
           016-01-01 (an earlier `start_date` returns `{"error":true,"rea
           son":"Parameter 'start_date' is out of allowed range from 2016
           -01-01 to ..."}`), **but all values are null until 2017-12-31*
           * (RK17). One request for 2016-01-01..2026-09-22 returned 3,91
           8 days (109 KB) in a single response, so no chunking is needed
           . Same grid cell and values as the forecast endpoint's `past_d
           ays`.
      206  - **Open-Meteo previous-runs (adopted in D20):** `https://prev
           ious-runs-api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast?...&hourly=precipitat
           ion,precipitation_previous_day1,precipitation_previous_day2&ti
           mezone=America%2FNew_York`. **Hourly only**: `daily=precipitat
           ion_sum_previous_day1` returns `{"error":true,"reason":"Invali
           d value: ... precipitation_sum_previous_day1"}`. Same grid cel
           l as the forecast endpoint. The first non-null values are `pre
           vious_day1` 2024-01-19T08:00, `previous_day2` 2024-01-20T08:00
           , `previous_day3` 2024-01-21T08:00, so all of 2024 from Februa
           ry onward and all of 2025–2026 are covered. On 2025-07-06 (Cha
           ntal), `previous_day1` = 87.8 mm and `previous_day2` = 5.8 mm.
      207 +- **NOAA MRMS via IEM (D24):** `https://mesonet.agron.iastate.
          +edu/iemre/multiday/2025-01-01/2025-12-31/36.0711/-79.0956/json
          +` returns `{"iemre_domain":"conus","iemre_i":375,"iemre_j":105
          +,"data":[{"date":"2025-07-06","mrms_precip_in":7.01,"prism_pre
          +cip_in":...,...}]}`. `mrms_precip_in` is in **inches**. Days f
          +ollow Central Standard Time (RK18b). One year per request (RK1
          +8a). Checked values: 2025-07-05 0.0, **07-06 7.01**, 07-07 0.0
          +8, 07-09 1.55, 07-10 0.35 in; 2026-09-18 **0.0** (Open-Meteo l
          +ead-0 said 51.5 mm); 2026-09-21 1.66 in (≈42 mm; Open-Meteo 47
          +.2 mm). NOAA's own archive (`https://noaa-mrms-pds.s3.amazonaw
          +s.com/CONUS/MultiSensor_QPE_24H_Pass2_00.00/YYYYMMDD/`) has 24
          + hourly GRIB2 files per day of about 4.5 MB each, starting 202
          +0-10-14; not used (D24).                                      
      208  - **Open-Meteo archive (not used):** the default model fills t
           hrough **today** (a partial day); with `models=era5` it is nul

           D2 and RK9 exist.

      295  ### Acceptance criteria
      296 -- [ ] AC-1.1 On an empty `DATA_DIR`, `make ingest` backfills U
          -SGS and historical-forecast weather from `HISTORY_START`, and 
          -previous-runs precipitation from `LEADS_START`, through yester
          -day (America/New_York), and writes one forecast file. That is 
          -four new files: `raw/usgs/usgs_<ts>.csv`, `raw/weather_hist/we
          -ather_hist_<ts>.csv`, `raw/weather_leads/weather_leads_<ts>.cs
          -v`, `raw/weather_fc/weather_fc_<ts>.csv`.                     
      296 +- [ ] AC-1.1 On an empty `DATA_DIR`, `make ingest` backfills U
          +SGS and historical-forecast weather from `HISTORY_START`, and 
          +previous-runs precipitation from `LEADS_START`, through yester
          +day (America/New_York), and writes one forecast file, plus MRM
          +S from `HISTORY_START` when `MRMS_ENABLED=1`. That is five new
          + files: `raw/usgs/usgs_<ts>.csv`, `raw/weather_hist/weather_hi
          +st_<ts>.csv`, `raw/weather_leads/weather_leads_<ts>.csv`, `raw
          +/weather_fc/weather_fc_<ts>.csv`, `raw/mrms/mrms_<ts>.csv`.   
      297  - [ ] AC-1.2 On a second run, the USGS request starts at `max(
           existing date) − USGS_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, and the historical-forec
           ast and previous-runs requests start at `max(existing date) + 
           1`. When nothing is new for a source, no request is made and n
           o file is written for it. The log states each requested range.
      298  - [ ] AC-1.3 USGS CSV columns: `date, flow_cfs, approval_statu
           s, qualifier, last_modified`. Sorted ascending by date even wh
           en the API returns rows unordered. `flow_cfs` is float. `quali
           fier` is a `;`-joined string or empty. Only `statistic_id=0000
           3` is requested.
      299  - [ ] AC-1.4 Weather CSV columns: `date, precip_mm, tmax_c, tm
           in_c, source, fetched_at_utc`, with `source ∈ {historical_fore
           cast, forecast}`. The forecast file holds the 7 past days plus
            3 forecast days. If the forecast endpoint returns fewer than 
           3 future days (today onward) or null values, the file is still
            written with what came back, and a WARNING `forecast returned
            N of 3 days` is logged (Forecast decides; AC-4.7). Every requ
           est uses `timezone=America/New_York`.

           at 60 s.
      301  - [ ] AC-1.6 On final failure (HTTP error, Open-Meteo `{"error
           ": true}` body, or malformed JSON), ingest logs an ERROR namin
           g the source and reason, writes no partial file for that sourc
           e, still attempts the other sources, and exits non-zero. An em
           pty USGS response (`features: []`) is logged as WARNING, write
           s no file, and is not a crash.

          +ge ingest's exit status**. Verification data must never block

      310  - [ ] AC-1.10 **Timezone pinning.** Every Open-Meteo request (
           forecast, historical-forecast, previous-runs) includes `timezo
           ne=America/New_York`, taken from `TIMEZONE`. Ingest checks tha
           t the response's `timezone` field equals the requested value; 
           on a mismatch it logs an ERROR `timezone mismatch: requested A
           merica/New_York, got <tz>` and writes no file for that source.
            USGS `time` and Open-Meteo `daily.time` are written verbatim 
           as `YYYY-MM-DD` strings: never parsed as timestamps, never loc
           alized, never converted to UTC.

           s_<ts>.csv` has columns `date, lead_days, precip_mm, n_hours, 
           fetched_at_utc`, with `lead_days ∈ {1, 2}`, from hourly `preci
           pitation_previous_day1` and `precipitation_previous_day2` requ
           ested with `timezone=America/New_York`. `precip_mm` is the sum
            of the hourly values on that local date. It is written only w
           hen every hour of that date is present and non-null (`n_hours`
            equals 23, 24, or 25 on DST-change days, as appropriate); oth
           erwise it is NaN, and a WARNING counts the incomplete days.
      312  - [ ] AC-1.8 Files are written atomically (write to `.tmp`, th
           en rename), so a crash never leaves a half-written CSV that ma
           tches the pattern.

> does this help?

● Yes, MRMS helps. If you meant to attach something with "does this help?", it
  didn't come through, so this answer is about the MRMS suggestion. Both
  additions are now in docs/plan.md.

  How MRMS helps. I checked it against Open-Meteo and the gauge on three storms:

  ┌─────────────────┬───────────────────┬──────────┬──────────────────────┐
  │      Date       │    Open-Meteo     │   MRMS   │        Gauge         │
  │                 │   same-day rain   │          │                      │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ 2026-09-18      │ 51.5 mm           │ 0.0 mm   │ flat, so Open-Meteo  │
  │                 │                   │          │ was wrong            │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ 2026-09-21      │ 47.2 mm           │ about 42 │ small rise, so the   │
  │                 │                   │  mm      │ two agree            │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ 2025-07-06      │ 54.7 mm           │ about    │ 15.5 to 1,990 cfs    │
  │ (Chantal)       │                   │ 178 mm   │ the same day         │
  └─────────────────┴───────────────────┴──────────┴──────────────────────┘

  The Chantal row suggests Open-Meteo badly under-forecast the storm.

  How it's used: verification only, never a model input. Keeping it out of the
  model means the forecast never depends on it and its inputs stay what the
  brief specifies. It's logged as decision D24.
  - Source: I get MRMS through Iowa State's IEM service as small daily JSON
    point values, back to 2018. NOAA's own archive is about 4.5 MB of GRIB2 per
    hour, starts only in October 2020, and would need about 10 GB plus extra
    libraries to backfill.
  - Ingest (AC-1.11): fetches one year per request. A single long request
    silently returns all nulls, so an all-null response counts as a failure. An
    MRMS failure never changes ingest's exit status.
  - Rain check (AC-2.9): Features writes rain_check_<ts>.json comparing
    Open-Meteo rain at 0, 1 and 2 days ahead against MRMS. It reports bias,
    average error, hits, misses and false alarms, plus the 10 days where they
    disagree most. A test checks that 2026-09-18 is counted as a false alarm,
    and another that no MRMS column ever reaches the model's features.
  - Alignment test (AC-2.5): also checks that MRMS's peak day (2025-07-06)
    matches the Open-Meteo heavy-rain day and the flow-jump day.
  - Poor-model protocol (D23): the diagnosis step now checks the rain check
    first, which separates bad weather input from a bad model.
  - Known quirks (RK18):
    - IEM's day follows Central Standard Time, 1–2 hours behind the Eastern day.
    - The value is for one grid cell of about 12 km, not a basin average.
    - IEM is a university service, not an official NOAA endpoint.

    None of these matter for a verification check.

  Flowchart: it's under "Architecture overview" in the Project section. It
  shows:
  - the five external sources feeding the pipeline container;
  - the scheduler order inside the container, and when training runs;
  - the named volume sfdata and its four folders;
  - the dashboard container reading those folders read-only, with its
    healthcheck, on to the browser;
  - Stage 0 as the base for both containers.

  I haven't rendered it. Check it in VS Code's Markdown preview (needs a Mermaid
  extension) or on GitHub after you push.

  Still open: the rain-day / dry-day split of the skill scores. MRMS would now
  make "rain day" easy to define (for example, MRMS at least 10 mm). Say yes if
  you want it.

  How the split works (new AC-3.10, logged as D25, your decision):
  - Labels: each 2025-onward test forecast is labelled by the rain MRMS actually
    observed over its forecast window.
    - Wet: 10 mm or more.
    - Dry: under 2 mm.
    - Light: anything in between, counted but not scored.
  - Scores: for each horizon, the metrics file reports skill against persistence
    and 80%/95% coverage separately for wet and dry days.
  - Visible in make coverage: the table shows these too, printed but not
    pass/fail.
  - Kept out of the model:
    - Features writes MRMS to its own file, features/mrms_<ts>.csv, which Train
      reads only to label rows after predicting.
    - A test checks that the saved model's inputs never include MRMS.
    - If MRMS is missing, the split is left empty and training still succeeds.
  - What to expect: skill near zero on dry days, any real skill showing up on
    wet days, and lower coverage on wet days.
