> how did you decide wet/dry split?

● Checking how many 2025 days would fall into each group, since I chose the
  thresholds by judgment and haven't tested them on data yet.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● I set the 10 mm and 2 mm cutoffs by judgment, using common conventions. I
  didn't tune them on data. I've now checked them against 2025 MRMS rain and the
  groups come out usable.

  The reasoning:
  - Wet at 10 mm or more matches a standard climate index: R10mm, the "heavy
    precipitation day" count (days with at least 10 mm). It's also about where
    the Eno starts to respond. On 2026-09-21, about 42 mm on dry soil still only
    lifted flow from 4 to 16.5 cfs, so much less than 10 mm usually does
    nothing.
  - Dry below 2 mm. The usual "wet day" cutoff is 1 mm, but I used 2 mm to
    absorb MRMS noise and the fact that the value is one 12 km grid cell rather
    than a basin average. Below that, rain produces essentially no runoff.
  - Light (2–10 mm) is left out of the scoring, so each group is clearly one or
    the other rather than split at an arbitrary line. It's still counted.
  - The window is d0+1 through d0+h, the rain the model had to forecast. Rain on
    d0 is already known at issue time and is one of the model's inputs.
  - The label comes from MRMS, not Open-Meteo. Labelling with the rain being
    evaluated would be circular: a missed storm would be labelled "dry" and hide
    the miss.

  Check on 2025 MRMS (the test period starts in 2025):

  ┌─────────┬─────┬─────┬────────────────────┐
  │ Horizon │ Wet │ Dry │ Light (not scored) │
  ├─────────┼─────┼─────┼────────────────────┤
  │ t+1     │ 39  │ 284 │ 41                 │
  ├─────────┼─────┼─────┼────────────────────┤
  │ t+2     │ 75  │ 235 │ 53                 │
  ├─────────┼─────┼─────┼────────────────────┤
  │ t+3     │ 105 │ 193 │ 64                 │
  └─────────┴─────┴─────┴────────────────────┘

  The wet group grows with horizon because longer windows collect more rain.
  With 2026 added, t+1 should have about 65 wet cases, which is small but enough
  for skill. Coverage from 39–65 cases is noisy: one standard error on 80%
  coverage is about ±0.06. That's another reason the split is reported but not
  pass/fail.

  Known weakness: wet days on dry soil barely raise the river, so the wet group
  mixes real events with non-events. A split by how much flow actually changed
  would be sharper, but it would use the outcome to choose the groups, so it's
  biased. I'd keep the rain-based split.

  If you want different cutoffs (say 1 and 25 mm), tell me before the Builder
  starts and I'll log them as your decision. Otherwise the plan stays as
  written.

  Handoff

  Role · Stage: Architect · done
  Changed: none
  Evidence: counts from the 2025 MRMS series (IEM, converted to mm), shown in
  the table. 2025 had 103 days with at least 1 mm, 39 with at least 10 mm, and
  15 with at least 25 mm.
  Status: 64 acceptance criteria, unchanged
  Needs a decision from you: keep 10 and 2 mm (my recommendation) or set your
  own
  Next step: commit, export the transcript, start the Builder

> I had codex run a review:
  Reviewed [docs/plan.md](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS7
  06-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md). I found these substantial issues:
  1. The leakage/as-of-time contract is incomplete.
     R6 and AC-2.8 define lead matching for future precipitation, but they do
  not clearly specify the issue-time source/availability for precip_0, api_7,
  api_30, and temperatures. The leakage test may pass while reconstructed
  historical values still differ from what was available at issuance.
     Recommendation: add a feature-by-split matrix specifying source, lead
  time, and allowed data cutoff; test each feature against that matrix.
  ([plan.md (line 16)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-
  StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:16), [plan.md (line 427)](C:/Users/Grace/Document
  s/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:427))
  2. Stage failure and stale-data behavior is ambiguous.
     Ingest may partially fail while the scheduler continues into features,
  training, and forecasting. Those stages could combine fresh weather with
  stale flow or stale historical weather. This conflicts somewhat with the
  “every stage succeeded” exit rule.
     Recommendation: define hard vs. soft failures and require a
  freshness/status manifest before downstream stages run. ([plan.md (line
  311)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/
  docs/plan.md:311), [plan.md (line 720)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_
  Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:720))
  3. Published artifacts are not consistently atomic or version-linked.
     Ingest requires atomic writes, but features, model/metrics pairs, and
  forecasts do not. The dashboard also independently selects the newest
  forecast, features, and metrics files, so it could display a mixed snapshot
  after a crash or retrain.
     Recommendation: use a run/snapshot ID and an atomic published manifest or
  marker; require dashboard artifacts to share that ID. ([plan.md (line
  322)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/
  docs/plan.md:322), [plan.md (line 496)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_
  Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:496), [plan.md (line
  649)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/
  docs/plan.md:649))
  4. The real-data coverage gate is not an actionable acceptance criterion.
     Training publishes even when skill or coverage fails, while make coverage
  exits nonzero based on fixed test thresholds. It is unclear whether that
  means the project is failing, or merely reporting an honest model limitation.
     Recommendation: make real-data coverage report-only, with a separate
  strict gate for deterministic synthetic/calibration checks; define the
  required response to poor test results. ([plan.md (line 500)](C:/Users/Grace/
  Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:500),
  [plan.md (line 510)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-
  StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:510))
  5. Fixture tests depend on the wall clock.
     Fixtures are dated around 2026-09-23, but production logic requires
  fc_fetched_date == local_today(). As the project or CI runs later,
  fixture-mode forecast tests can fail for reasons unrelated to the code.
     Recommendation: inject an as_of_date/clock in fixture and test mode while
  retaining real-time behavior by default. ([plan.md (line 17)](C:/Users/Grace/
  Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:17),
  [plan.md (line 586)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-
  StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:586))
  6. External API and dependency versions are not pinned strongly enough.
     Open-Meteo’s default model behavior, response schema, and units are not
  pinned or recorded. Pagination is mentioned as a risk but lacks an acceptance
  test, and exact dependency versions are deferred to implementation.
     Recommendation: pin API model parameters and dependency versions, record
  source metadata, and validate response identity, units, array lengths, date
  coverage, and pagination. ([plan.md (line 121)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall
  26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:121), [plan.md (line
  159)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/
  docs/plan.md:159))
  7. The fallback-model decision conflicts with the stated scope.
     R8 and the out-of-scope section prohibit model comparison, but D23 permits
  trying an alternative model.
     Recommendation: resolve this before implementation—either remove D23 or
  explicitly permit one predeclared fallback with fixed selection rules.
  ([plan.md (line 19)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-
  StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:19), [plan.md (line 141)](C:/Users/Grace/Document
  s/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/docs/plan.md:141))
  8. Metric edge cases are underspecified.
     The plan does not define NSE behavior for constant/near-constant
  observations, minimum sample sizes, empty wet/dry groups, or the exact
  common-row policy for model versus persistence metrics.
     Recommendation: specify formulas and failure behavior before
  implementation so metrics cannot silently become misleading. ([plan.md (line
  497)](C:/Users/Grace/Documents/Fall26/Data_Engineering/IDS706-StreamForecast/
  docs/plan.md:497))
  No files were changed.

● I checked each Codex finding against the plan. Most are fair. Two ask for more
  machinery than this project needs; the underlying problem can be fixed more
  cheaply.

  #: 1
  Finding: No availability contract for past-weather features (precip_0, api_7,
  api_30, temperatures)
  Valid?: Partly. The data are fine: at run time, past days come from the
  forecast endpoint's past_days, which I verified equals historical-forecast.
  But the plan never says so.
  Recommendation: Accept. Add a table with one row per feature: source per split

  (train, calibration, test, live), days ahead, and the latest date it may use.
   Tests check against it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Finding: Partial ingest failure, then later stages mix fresh and stale data
  Valid?: Partly. Forecast already refuses on stale flow or weather (AC-4.5,
  4.10). What's unclear is AC-6.9's "exit 0 only if every stage succeeded"
  versus MRMS being allowed to fail.
  Recommendation: Accept a reduced fix. State which failures stop the run (hard)

  and which only warn (soft): source failures inside ingest are soft, and
  Forecast's freshness checks are the gate. Decline the status file.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Finding: Only ingest writes files safely; the dashboard can mix files from
  different runs
  Valid?: Yes.
  Recommendation: Accept a reduced fix. Every stage writes files atomically. The

  dashboard loads the metrics file that matches the forecast's model_version
  instead of simply the newest one. Decline run IDs and a manifest.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Finding: Can't tell whether make coverage failing means the project fails
  Valid?: Yes, a real ambiguity.
  Recommendation: Accept. AC-3.9 checks that the coverage check works correctly
  (hand-written test files). A real-data result outside the tolerances becomes
  a finding under D23, not an unmet criterion. Keep the non-zero exit so it's
  visible.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Finding: Offline fixture runs depend on today's date
  Valid?: Yes, an important bug. A fixture-mode run would show 2026-09-22 data
  as
  several days stale and refuse.
  Recommendation: Accept. Add an AS_OF_DATE setting (empty means real today),
  used by paths.local_today(). Fixture-mode smoke tests and CI set it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 6
  Finding: API versions, units and pagination not pinned
  Valid?: Partly. Exact package versions are pinned at Stage 0 by design; I
  can't
  know them before installing.
  Recommendation: Accept: check units (mm, °C, ft^3/s), the returned timezone,
  array lengths and date coverage; record the grid cell and elevation in raw
  files so a change is noticed; add a test for USGS's next-page link. Decline
  naming an Open-Meteo model: the verified default is what we match on.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 7
  Finding: D23 contradicts "no model comparison"
  Valid?: Yes, wording.
  Recommendation: Accept. Make R8 and Out of scope say "except the single
  pre-declared alternative in D23."
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 8
  Finding: Metric edge cases undefined
  Valid?: Yes.
  Recommendation: Accept. NSE is null when observed variance is 0. Any group
  with
  fewer than 20 rows reports null, marked "insufficient". Model and persistence
   are scored on exactly the same rows. Formulas written out.

  Why I'd decline the run-ID manifest and status-file parts of #2 and #3: the
  brief says "smallest design that meets the goal." Atomic writes, Forecast's
  freshness checks, and matching the dashboard to model_version fix the actual
  failures at a fraction of the complexity.

  Apply all of the above as recommended? Each item would be logged as a Student
  decision citing the Codex review. Or name any you want handled differently.

      324    - `MRMS_ENABLED=0` skips it entirely.
      325  - [ ] AC-1.10 **Timezone pinning.** Every Open-Meteo request (
           forecast, historical-forecast, previous-runs) includes `timezo
           ne=America/New_York`, taken from `TIMEZONE`. Ingest checks tha
           t the response's `timezone` field equals the requested value; 
           on a mismatch it logs an ERROR `timezone mismatch: requested A
           merica/New_York, got <tz>` and writes no file for that source.
            USGS `time` and Open-Meteo `daily.time` are written verbatim 
           as `YYYY-MM-DD` strings: never parsed as timestamps, never loc
           alized, never converted to UTC.
      326  - [ ] AC-1.9 Previous-runs CSV `raw/weather_leads/weather_lead
           s_<ts>.csv` has columns `date, lead_days, precip_mm, n_hours, 
           fetched_at_utc`, with `lead_days ∈ {1, 2}`, from hourly `preci
           pitation_previous_day1` and `precipitation_previous_day2` requ
           ested with `timezone=America/New_York`. `precip_mm` is the sum
            of the hourly values on that local date. It is written only w
           hen every hour of that date is present and non-null (`n_hours`
            equals 23, 24, or 25 on DST-change days, as appropriate); oth
           erwise it is NaN, and a WARNING counts the incomplete days.
      327 -- [ ] AC-1.8 Files are written atomically (write to `.tmp`, th
          -en rename), so a crash never leaves a half-written CSV that ma
          -tches the pattern.                                            
      327 +- [ ] AC-1.8 Files are written atomically with `paths.atomic_w
          +rite` (AC-0.9), so a crash never leaves a half-written CSV tha
          +t matches the pattern.                                        
      328 +- [ ] AC-1.12 **Response validation (D26).** Before writing, i
          +ngest checks each response; a failure is handled as a source f
          +ailure (AC-1.6).                                              
      329 +  - **Units:**                                                
      330 +    - Open-Meteo `daily_units` must be `mm` for precipitation 
          +and `°C` for temperatures.                                    
      331 +    - Previous-runs `hourly_units` must be `mm`.              
      332 +    - Every USGS row must have `unit_of_measure == "ft^3/s"`, 
          +`statistic_id == "00003"`, and `monitoring_location_id == "USG
          +S-" + USGS_SITE`. Non-matching USGS rows are dropped and count
          +ed, with an ERROR if any are dropped.                         
      333 +  - **Shape:** every data array has the same length as `time`.
      334 +  - **Date coverage:** returned dates are contiguous and cover
          + the requested range. Missing dates are listed in a WARNING an
          +d the file is still written.                                  
      335 +  - **Grid cell:** weather CSVs also record `grid_lat`, `grid_
          +lon`, `elevation` from the response. Features logs a WARNING i
          +f the grid cell differs between files.                        
      336 +  - **Pagination:** USGS follows every `next` link until none

            3 forecast days. If the forecast endpoint returns fewer than

           est uses `timezone=America/New_York`.

      525  - [ ] AC-3.1 Splits come from `TRAIN_END`, `CALIBRATION_START`
           , and `CALIBRATION_END`, by date: train covers ..`TRAIN_END`, 
           calibration covers `CALIBRATION_START`..`CALIBRATION_END`, and
            test covers after `CALIBRATION_END`. Rows between `TRAIN_END`
            and `CALIBRATION_START` are unused. A row belongs to a split 
           only if both d0 and d0+h fall inside it. Every calibration and
            test row has `weather_lead_matched = True`; training aborts w
           ith an error if not. Rows with a NaN feature are dropped from 
           calibration and test and counted in the log (HGB would accept 
           them, but they would not be lead-matched). The three splits do
            not overlap and are strictly ordered in time, and no shufflin
           g occurs anywhere (test asserts max(train date) < min(calibrat
           ion date) < min(test date) per horizon).
      526  - [ ] AC-3.2 Three models (h = 1, 2, 3) with `random_state=0`

           , `flow_cfs`, `approval_status`, `qualifier`, `flow_filled`, `
           weather_lead_matched`, `fc_fetched_date`, and the targets).
      527  - [ ] AC-3.3 The residual quantiles at 0.025, 0.10, 0.50, 0.90

      528  - [ ] AC-3.4 Checkpoint `data/models/model_<ts>.joblib` contai
           ns `models`, `residual_quantiles`, `feature_columns`, `sklearn
           _version`, `split_dates`, and `trained_at_utc`. Sidecar `data/
           models/metrics_<ts>.json` has the same `<ts>`, and both store 
           `model_version = <ts>`. Both are written with `paths.atomic_wr
           ite` (AC-0.9), the checkpoint first and the sidecar last; a ch
           eckpoint without a sidecar counts as unpublished. The sidecar 
           is valid JSON with no `NaN`/`Infinity` (`allow_nan=False`); mi

      529  - [ ] AC-3.5 The sidecar reports, per horizon on the test spli
           t: `n`, model `mae_cfs`, `rmse_cfs`, `nse`; persistence `mae_c
           fs`, `nse`; `skill_mae = 1 − mae_model/mae_persistence`; `cove
           rage_80`, `coverage_95`; and `coverage_80_high`, `coverage_95_
           high`, `n_high` for days whose observed flow at d0+h exceeds t
           he training 90th percentile of flow. Calibration-split coverag
           e is reported too.
      530 +  **Metric definitions (D26), for every group (overall, high-f
          +low, wet, dry):**                                             
      531 +  - **Common rows:** model and persistence are scored on exact
          +ly the same rows: those with an observed target and complete f
          +eatures (`n` counts them).                                    
      532 +  - **Formulas** (o = observed cfs at d0+h, p = prediction in 
          +cfs):                                                         
      533 +    - `mae = mean|o−p|`                                       
      534 +    - `rmse = sqrt(mean (o−p)²)`                              
      535 +    - `nse = 1 − Σ(o−p)² / Σ(o−ō)²`                           
      536 +    - `skill_mae = 1 − mae_model / mae_persistence`           
      537 +    - `coverage_x` = share of rows with `lo_x ≤ o ≤ hi_x`     
      538 +  - **Edge cases:**                                           
      539 +    - `nse` is `null` when `Σ(o−ō)² = 0`.                     
      540 +    - `skill_mae` is `null` when `mae_persistence = 0`.       
      541 +    - A group with `n < 20` reports every metric as `null` wit
          +h `"status": "insufficient"`, otherwise `"status": "ok"`; an e
          +mpty group has `n = 0`.                                       
      542 +  - **Never silent:** no metric is ever computed from mismatch
          +ed rows or reported as `NaN`.                                 
      543  - [ ] AC-3.6 On a synthetic stationary series (fixed seed, gen
           erated in the test), calibration and test coverage of the 80% 
           band are within 0.80 ± 0.05 and of the 95% band within 0.95 ± 
           0.03, and the model beats persistence (`skill_mae > 0`) at h =
            1.

           n on real data as a finding.

      141  | D23 | **Protocol if the model performs poorly**, fixed befor
           e any results are seen. (1) **Diagnose first:** check join dat

            features match between training and forecasting, and the MRMS

           her-input problem, not the model; bugs are fixed to match the

   decide whether to publish. Each pass logs a one-line summary, and make
  pipeline exits with Forecast's exit code.
  Where: AC-6.10 (AC-6.9 wording changed)
  ────────────────────────────────────────

  helper. The dashboard shows the metrics for the model that made the displayed
   forecast, not simply the newest metrics file.

  means a bug; fix it. make coverage is not part of make test or CI.
  Where: AC-3.9, targets table
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  What changed: New AS_OF_DATE setting for fixture runs, tests and CI. Fixture
  mode requires it and marks fixture data as fetched at noon on that date. Real
